Appendix B: Proofs and Remarks

Throughout the appendix, we will use a simplified version of the notation introduced in
Section

II.2
. In particular, we will use the following notation:

•

\textbf{C}_{n}=\textbf{C}_{n}^{(1)}
and
\textbf{C}^{*}_{n}=\textbf{C}_{n}^{*(1)}
;

•

\textbf{D}_{n,g}=\textbf{D}_{n,g}^{(1)}
and
\textbf{D}^{*}_{n,g}=\textbf{D}_{n,g}^{*(1)}
with
g_{n,i}=g_{n,i}^{(1)}
;

•

\textbf{B}_{n}=\textbf{B}_{n}^{(2)}
and
\textbf{B}^{*}_{n}=\textbf{B}_{n}^{*(2)}
;

•

{D}_{n,k}={D}_{n,k}^{(2)}
and
{D}^{*}_{n,k}={D}_{n,k}^{*(2)}
with
k_{n,i}=k_{n,i}^{(2)}
.

B.1 Proofs and Remarks of
Section

II.2.2

Proof of
II.2.3
.

Proof of
II.2.3
(i)
:
First, we show that
II.2.2

(i)
-
(iv)
imply parts (i), (ii), and (iii) of
I.2.1
for
\textbf{h}_{n,i}(t,\bm{\beta})=\big(k_{n}(t,\bm{\beta}),\textbf{g}_{n,i}(t,\bm{\beta})^{\top}\big)^{\top}=\big(J_{n}(t)S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\bm{\beta}),(\textbf{Z}_{i}-\textbf{E}_{n}(t,{\bm{\beta}}))^{\top}\big)^{\top}
and analogously for its limit in probability
\tilde{\textbf{h}}_{i}(t,\bm{\beta})=

\big(\tilde{k}(t,\bm{\beta}),\tilde{\textbf{g}}_{i}(t,\bm{\beta})^{\top}\big)^{\top}=\big(s^{(0)}(t,\bm{\beta})^{-1},(\textbf{Z}_{i}-\textbf{e}(t,{\bm{\beta}}))^{\top}\big)^{\top}
,
t\in\mathcal{T}
and
\bm{\beta}\in\mathcal{B}
.
Let
\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n}
be a consistent estimator of
{\bm{{\beta}}_{0}}
. Because

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T},i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}}\lVert\big(k_{n}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n}),\textbf{g}_{n,i}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})^{\top}\big)^{\top}-\big(\tilde{k}(t,{\bm{{\beta}}_{0}}),\tilde{\textbf{g}}_{i}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})^{\top}\big)^{\top}\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lVert k_{n}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-\tilde{k}(t,{\bm{{\beta}}_{0}})\rVert_{\infty}+\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T},i\in\{1,\ldots,n\}}\lVert\textbf{g}_{n,i}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-\tilde{\textbf{g}}_{i}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})\rVert_{\infty},

it suffices for proving part (i) of
I.2.1
of Part I to consider the convergence of each of the two terms separately. Obviously, for proving the parts (ii) and (iii) we can also treat the two components of
h_{n,i}(t,\bm{\beta})
separately. Let us consider
k_{n}(t,\bm{\beta})
first.
We have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lvert k_{n}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-\tilde{k}(t,{\bm{{\beta}}_{0}})\rvert\\
&=\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lvert J_{n}(t)S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})^{-1}-s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})^{-1}\rvert\\
&=\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lvert(J_{n}(t)-1+1)\cdot(\frac{s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})}{S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})}-1+1)\cdot s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})^{-1}-s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})^{-1}\rvert\\
&=\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lvert\big[(J_{n}(t)-1)\cdot(\frac{s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})}{S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})}-1)+(J_{n}(t)-1)+(\frac{s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})}{S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})}-1)+1\big]\\
&\qquad\cdot s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})^{-1}-s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})^{-1}\rvert\\
&=\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lvert\big[(J_{n}(t)-1)\cdot(\frac{s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})}{S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})}-1)+(J_{n}(t)-1)+(\frac{s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})}{S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})}-1)\big]\\
&\qquad\cdot s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})^{-1}\rvert.\end{split}

(II.30)

Moreover, we know that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lvert\big(\frac{S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})}{s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})}-1\big)\frac{s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})}{s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})}\rvert

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lvert\big(S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})\big)s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})^{-1}\rvert

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\big\{\lvert[S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-s^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})+s^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-S_{n}^{(0)}(t,{\bm{\beta}}_{0})+S_{n}^{(0)}(t,{\bm{\beta}}_{0})-s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle\quad\cdot s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})^{-1}\rvert\big\}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\big\{\lvert S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-s^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})\rvert+\lvert S_{n}^{(0)}(t,{\bm{\beta}}_{0})-s^{(0)}(t,{\bm{{\beta}}_{0}})+s^{(0)}(t,{\bm{\beta}}_{0})-s^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})\rvert

\displaystyle\quad+\lvert S_{n}^{(0)}(t,{\bm{\beta}}_{0})-s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})\rvert\big\}\cdot\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lvert s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})^{-1}\rvert

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

The above convergence in probability to zero, as
n\rightarrow\infty
, holds for any consistent estimator
\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n}\in\mathcal{B}
of
\bm{{\beta}}_{0}
due to
II.2.2

(i)
, the continuity of

in
\bm{\beta}\in\mathcal{B}
(
II.2.2

(ii)
), and the boundedness of

for all

according to
II.2.2

(iii)
&
(iv)
, see (
II.6

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lvert\frac{s^{(0)}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})}{S_{n}^{(0)}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})}-1\rvert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,

(II.31)

for any consistent estimator

Next, we derive from which conditions of

I.2.3

(i)
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lVert\nabla k_{n}(t,\check{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-\tilde{\textbf{K}}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})\rVert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,

(II.36)

for any consistent estimator

Section

Proof of
II.2.3
(ii)
:
We derive the limit in probability of
\textbf{C}_{n}
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Note that

\langle\textbf{D}_{n,g}\rangle(t)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\textbf{Z}_{i}-\textbf{E}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})\big)^{\otimes 2}d\Lambda_{i}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\textbf{R}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})d\Lambda_{i}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})
.
Hence, we have

\frac{1}{n}\textbf{I}_{n}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})-\langle\textbf{D}_{n,g}\rangle(t)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\textbf{R}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})dM_{i}(u),

where the right-hand side of the equation above is a local square integrable martingale, according to Proposition II.4.1 of
Andersen et al. 1993
. Following the notation introduced in Part I, we denote this martingale by
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\textbf{D}_{n,R}(t)
. Under
II.2.2

(i)
-
(iv)
it follows from
I.2.2
of Part I that
\langle\textbf{D}_{n,R}\rangle(t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\langle\textbf{D}_{r}\rangle(t)
for all
t\in\mathcal{T}
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
, where
\langle\textbf{D}_{r}\rangle(t)
is some covariance function bounded for all
t\in\mathcal{T}
. Thus,
\frac{1}{n}\langle\textbf{D}_{n,R}\rangle(\tau)
and likewise the corresponding martingale
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\textbf{D}_{n,R}
converge to zero in probability, as
n\rightarrow\infty
, according to Lenglart’s Inequality. In other words,
\frac{1}{n}\textbf{I}_{n}(\tau,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})
and
\langle\textbf{D}_{n,g}\rangle(\tau)
are asymptotically equivalent and we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\textbf{I}_{n}(\tau,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})=\langle\textbf{D}_{n,g}\rangle(\tau)+o_{p}(1)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\langle\textbf{D}_{\tilde{g}}\rangle(\tau)=\textbf{V}_{\tilde{g}}(\tau),\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,

with
\textbf{V}_{\tilde{g}}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\textbf{r}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})s^{(0)}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})dA_{1;0}(u)
.
By the continuous mapping theorem and because
\frac{1}{n}\textbf{I}_{n}(\tau,\bm{\beta}_{0})
is asymptotically invertible under
II.2.2

(v)
, it follows from
II.2.2
that

\displaystyle\textbf{C}_{n}=\big(\frac{1}{n}\textbf{I}_{n}(\tau,\bm{\beta}_{0})\big)^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\textbf{V}_{\tilde{g}}(\tau)^{-1}=\textbf{C},\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

(II.37)

Hence,
I.2.5
of Part I is satisfied under
II.2.2
.

Recall that the wild bootstrap counterpart
\textbf{C}^{*}_{n}
of
\textbf{C}_{n}=\big(\frac{1}{n}\textbf{I}_{n}(\tau,\bm{\beta}_{0})\big)^{-1}
is defined through the optional covariation process
[\textbf{D}^{*}_{n,g}](\tau)
of
\textbf{D}^{*}_{n,g}
, in this case as

\displaystyle\textbf{C}^{*}_{n}=\big([\textbf{D}^{*}_{n,g}](\tau)\big)^{-1}=\big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{\tau}(\bm{Z}_{i}-\bm{E}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}))^{\otimes 2}G_{i}^{2}dN_{i}(u)\big)^{-1};

(II.38)

cf.
I.3.2
of Part I.
The particular choice of
\textbf{C}^{*}_{n}
is motivated by the fact that, under
II.2.2

(i)
-
(iv)
and conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)
, we have
[\textbf{D}^{*}_{n,g}](t)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}[\textbf{D}_{\tilde{g}}](t)=\textbf{V}_{\tilde{g}}(t)
for all
t\in\mathcal{T}
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, according to
I.3.7
of Part I. Hence, from the continuous mapping theorem and because of the asymptotic invertibility of
\textbf{V}_{\tilde{g}}(\tau)
according to
II.2.2

(v)
it follows under
II.2.2
that

\displaystyle\textbf{C}^{*}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\textbf{V}_{\tilde{g}}(\tau)^{-1}=\textbf{C},\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

(II.39)

From (
II.37
) and (
II.39
) we conclude that

\lVert\textbf{C}_{n}^{*}-\textbf{C}_{n}\rVert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,\quad n\rightarrow\infty,

which is why
I.3.9
of Part I is fulfilled under
II.2.2
.
In conclusion, under
II.2.2
both
I.2.5
and
I.3.9
of Part I are satisfied.

Proof of
II.2.3
(iii)
:
We need to prove that under
II.2.2
, Condition VII.2.1 of
Andersen et al. 1993
holds. It is easy to see that
II.2.2

(i)
-
(iii)
and
II.2.2

(v)
are identical to Condition VII.2.1 (a) - (c)
and Condition VII.2.1 (e), respectively. Thus, it is only left to show that
II.2.2

(iv)
implies Condition VII.2.1 (d). In particular, we need to prove that under
II.2.2

(iv)
the following holds:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{\beta}}s^{(0)}(t,\bm{\beta})=\textbf{s}^{(1)}(t,\bm{\beta}),\quad\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\bm{\beta}^{2}}s^{(0)}(t,\bm{\beta})=\textbf{s}^{(2)}(t,\bm{\beta}),\text{ for }\bm{\beta}\in\mathcal{B},t\in\mathcal{T}.\end{split}

(II.40)

For this we recall (
II.6
), this is, under
II.2.2

(iv)
we have

\textbf{s}^{(m)}(t,\bm{\beta})=\mathbb{E}(Y_{1}(t)\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\otimes m}\exp(\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\beta})),

for all fixed
t\in\mathcal{T}
,
m\in\{0,1,2\}
(in non-bold-type for
m=0
), and
\bm{\beta}\in\mathcal{B}
.
Furthermore, we have

|\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{\beta}_{j}}Y_{1}(t)\exp(\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\beta})|=|Y_{1}(t)Z_{1j}\exp(\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\beta})|\leq\lvert Z_{1j}\rvert\exp(K)

and

|\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\bm{\beta}_{j}\partial\bm{\beta}_{l}}Y_{1}(t)\exp(\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\beta})|=|Y_{1}(t)Z_{1j}Z_{1l}\exp(\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\beta})|\leq\lvert Z_{1j}Z_{1l}\rvert\exp(K),

where
Z_{1j}
is the
j
-th component of
\textbf{Z}_{1}
,
j,l=1,\ldots,q
.
Note that
K
is bounded due to the boundedness of the covariates and the boundedness of
\mathcal{B}
, so that the bounds on the right-hand side of the two formulas above are integrable random variables.
According to Theorem 12.5 of
Schilling 2005
, it then follows that the integral and the differential operator can be interchanged, which yields

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{\beta}_{j}}s^{(0)}(t,\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\bm{\beta}_{j}}Y_{1}(t)\exp(\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\beta}))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(Y_{1}(t)Z_{1j}\exp(\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\beta})),

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\bm{\beta}_{j}\partial\bm{\beta}_{l}}s^{(0)}(t,\bm{\beta})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\bm{\beta}_{j}\partial\bm{\beta}_{l}}Y_{1}(t)\exp(\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\beta}))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(Y_{1}(t)Z_{1j}Z_{1l}\exp(\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\beta})),

for
j,l=1,\ldots,q
. Hence, the gradient and the Hessian matrix of
s^{(0)}(t,\bm{\beta})
are given by

\textbf{s}^{(1)}(t,\bm{\beta})=\mathbb{E}(Y_{1}(t)\textbf{Z}_{1}\exp(\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\beta})),\quad\textbf{s}^{(2)}(t,\bm{\beta})=\mathbb{E}(Y_{1}(t)\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\otimes 2}\exp(\textbf{Z}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\beta})),

for all fixed
t\in\mathcal{T}
and
\bm{\beta}\in\mathcal{B}
, respectively, so that (
II.40
) holds under
II.2.2

(iv)
. Hence, Condition VII.2.1 of
Andersen et al. 1993
follows from
II.2.2
. This completes the proof of
II.2.3
.
\blacksquare

Remark II.6.1
.

As explained in
II.2.1
and mentioned in
Fine and Gray 1999
, the structures related to the Fine-Gray model coincide with those under the Cox model. In particular, this holds for the log Cox partial likelihood and the log partial likelihood under the Fine-Gray model. Thus, by means of
II.2.3

(iii)
we resort to Theorem VII.2.1 of
Andersen et al. 1993
for the Cox model in which it is shown via the log Cox partial likelihood that
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}
is unique with probability converging to 1 and that
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}
is a consistent estimator for
\bm{{\beta}}_{0}
.

Remark II.6.2
.

The score statistic
\bm{U}_{n}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})
is a local square integrable martingale in
t\in\mathcal{T}
.
In order to see this, we point out the following two observations

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\textbf{Z}_{i}d\Lambda_{i}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=n\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\textbf{Z}_{i}Y_{i}(u)\exp(\textbf{Z}_{i}^{\top}\bm{{\beta}}_{0})dA_{1;0}(u)

\displaystyle=n\int_{0}^{t}\textbf{S}^{(1)}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})dA_{1;0}(u)

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\textbf{E}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})d\Lambda_{i}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{\textbf{S}^{(1)}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})}{S^{(0)}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})}nS^{(0)}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})dA_{1;0}(u)

\displaystyle=n\int_{0}^{t}\textbf{S}^{(1)}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})dA_{1;0}(u).

Thus,
\bm{U}_{n}(\cdot,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})
can be expressed as integrals with respect to counting process martingales, i.e.,

\displaystyle\bm{U}_{n}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\bm{Z}_{i}-\bm{E}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})\big)(dM_{i}(u)+d\Lambda_{i}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\big(\bm{Z}_{i}-\bm{E}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})\big)dM_{i}(u)

with predictable and locally bounded integrands
\bm{Z}_{i}-\bm{E}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})
,
i=1,\ldots,n
. It follows with Proposition II.4.1 of
Andersen et al. 1993
that
\bm{U}_{n}(\cdot,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})
is a local square integrable martingale with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{1}
.

B.2 Proofs and Remarks of
Section

Remark II.6.3
.

According to the facts below, all assumptions necessary for the asymptotic representation (
I.11
) of Part I to hold are satisfied for
X_{n}^{(2)}=\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})
and
X^{(2)}=A_{1;0}
.

•

The integrand
k_{n}(t,\bm{\beta})=J_{n}(t)S^{(0)}_{n}(t,\bm{\beta})^{-1}
of
X_{n}^{(2)}
is almost surely continuously differentiable in
\bm{\beta}
by definition of
J_{n}(t)
and
S^{(0)}_{n}(t,\bm{\beta})
.

•

The regularity assumption (
I.5
) of Part I holds, since

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}k_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})d\Lambda_{i}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})-A_{1;0}(t)\big)=\sqrt{n}\big(\int_{0}^{t}(J_{n}(u)-1)dA_{1;0}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Here we have used
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}d\Lambda_{i}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})=S^{(0)}_{n}(t,{\bm{{\beta}}_{0}})dA_{1;0}
,
\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\sqrt{n}\lvert J_{n}(t)-1\rvert=o_{p}(1)
, and
A_{1;0}(\tau)<\infty
.

•

The asymptotic representation (
I.8
) of Part I is fulfilled because of (
II.11
), which has been derived under
II.2.2
by means of
II.2.3

(iii)
and Theorem VII.2.1 of
Andersen et al. 1993
.

•

The consistency assumption (
I.2
) of Part I, i.e.,
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}-\bm{\beta}_{0}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
holds under
II.2.2
according to
II.2.4
.

.

From the following facts we have that all assumptions necessary for (

) of Part I to hold are satisfied for

and

.

•

The integrand
k_{n}(t,\bm{\beta})=J_{n}(t)S^{(0)}_{n}(t,\bm{\beta})^{-1}
of
X_{n}^{(2)}
is almost surely continuously differentiable in
\bm{\beta}
by definition of
J_{n}(t)
and
S^{(0)}_{n}(t,\bm{\beta})
.

•

We use the same wild bootstrap representation for
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{*}_{n}-\hat{\bm{\beta}})
as in (
I.13
) of Part I, cf. (
II.22
).

•

\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{*}_{n}-\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}=O_{p}(n^{-1/2})
holds under

according to
II.2.6
.

•

The wild bootstrap estimator
\hat{A}^{*}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{*}_{n})
has been obtained by applying
I.3.1
of Part I to
\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})
, just like
X_{n}^{*(2)}
has been obtained based on
X_{n}^{(2)}
.

Proof of
II.2.8

We write

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})(\cdot)&=\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{\top}-\bm{{\beta}}_{0}^{\top},\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-A_{1;0}(\cdot))^{\top}\\
&=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}_{q\times 1}&+\textbf{I}_{q\times q}\cdot\textbf{C}_{n}\cdot\textbf{D}_{n,g}(\tau)&+o_{p}(1)\\
D_{n,k}(\cdot)&+\textbf{B}_{n}(\cdot)\cdot\textbf{C}_{n}\cdot\textbf{D}_{n,g}(\tau)&+o_{p}(1)\end{pmatrix}\\
&={\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{k}}(\cdot)+\check{\textbf{B}}_{n}(\cdot)\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}_{n}\cdot{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{g}}(\tau)+o_{p}(1),\end{split}

(II.41)

where
{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{k}}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\check{\textbf{k}}_{n}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})dM_{i}(u)
and
{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{g}}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\check{\textbf{g}}_{n,i}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})dM_{i}(u)
,
t\in\mathcal{T}
, with

\check{\textbf{k}}_{n}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}_{q\times 1}\\
k_{n}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\check{\textbf{g}}_{n,i}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})=\begin{pmatrix}{\textbf{g}}_{n,i}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})\\
{\textbf{g}}_{n,i}(t,\bm{{\beta}}_{0})\end{pmatrix},

and

\check{\textbf{B}}_{n}(t)=\begin{pmatrix}\textbf{I}_{q\times q}&\bm{0}_{q\times q}\\
\bm{0}_{1\times q}&\textbf{B}_{n}(t)\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\check{\textbf{C}}_{n}=\begin{pmatrix}\textbf{C}_{n}&\bm{0}_{q\times q}\\
\bm{0}_{q\times q}&\textbf{C}_{n}\end{pmatrix},

t\in\mathcal{T}
, where
\bm{0}_{q\times 1}
denotes the
q
-dimensional vector of zeros,
\bm{0}_{q\times q}
denotes the
q\times q
-dimensional matrix of zeros,
\textbf{I}_{q\times q}
denotes the
q\times q
-dimensional identity matrix, and
{\textbf{B}}_{n}
and
{\textbf{C}}_{n}
as given in (
II.15
) and (
II.10
), respectively.
The main consequence of (
II.41
) is that the particular structure of the asymptotic representation of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}-\bm{{\beta}}_{0})
and
\sqrt{n}(\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-A_{1;0}(\cdot))
carries over to the structure of the asymptotic representation of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})
. Additionally, the components
{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{k}}
,
{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{g}}
,
\check{\textbf{B}}_{n}
and
\check{\textbf{C}}_{n}
have the same properties as
{\textbf{D}}_{n,k}
,
{\textbf{D}}_{n,g}
,
{\textbf{B}}_{n}
and
{\textbf{C}}_{n}
. Especially,
{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{k}}
and
{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{g}}
are square integrable martingales with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{1}
, respectively, and under
II.2.2

(i)
-
(iv)

({\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{k}},{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{g}})
converges in law, as
n\rightarrow\infty
, to the zero-mean Gaussian vector martingale
({\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}},{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}})
with covariance function

\textbf{V}_{(\tilde{\check{k}},\tilde{\check{g}})}=\begin{pmatrix}\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}&\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{k}},\tilde{\check{g}}}\\
\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{g}},\tilde{\check{k}}}&\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}\end{pmatrix},

where

\displaystyle\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}(t)

\displaystyle=\langle\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}\rangle(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\check{\textbf{k}}}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\lambda_{1}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0}))du=\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\check{\textbf{k}}}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\otimes 2}s^{(0)}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})dA_{1;0}(u),

\displaystyle\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}(t)

\displaystyle=\langle\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}\rangle(t)=\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\check{\textbf{g}}}_{1}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\lambda_{1}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0}))du=\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{\check{\textbf{g}}}_{1}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\otimes 2}s^{(0)}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})dA_{1;0}(u),

with
\tilde{\check{\textbf{k}}}(t,\bm{\beta}_{0})=(\bm{0}_{q\times 1}^{\top},\tilde{k}(t,\bm{\beta}_{0}))^{\top}
,
\tilde{\check{\textbf{g}}}_{1}(t,\bm{\beta}_{0})=(\tilde{{\textbf{g}}}_{1}(t,\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top},\tilde{{\textbf{g}}}_{1}(t,\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top})^{\top}
,
and

\displaystyle\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{k}},\tilde{\check{g}}}(t)^{\top}=\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{g}},\tilde{\check{k}}}(t)

\displaystyle=\langle{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}},{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}\rangle\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\check{\textbf{g}}}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})\cdot\tilde{\check{\textbf{k}}}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})^{\top}\lambda_{1}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0}))du

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}(\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}_{q\times q}&\tilde{{\textbf{g}}}_{1}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})\cdot\tilde{{k}}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})\\
\bm{0}_{q\times q}&\tilde{{\textbf{g}}}_{1}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})\cdot\tilde{{k}}(u,\bm{\beta}_{0})\end{pmatrix}\lambda_{1}(u,\bm{{\beta}}_{0}))du

\displaystyle=\bm{0}_{2q\times(q+1)},

as
\textbf{V}_{\tilde{g},\tilde{k}}(t)=\bm{0}_{q\times 1}
by (
II.17
). In particular, the orthogonality of the Gaussian martingales
D_{\tilde{{k}}}
and
\textbf{D}_{\tilde{{g}}}
carries over to
\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}
and
\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}
.
Moreover, under
II.2.2
, the limits in probability of
\check{\textbf{B}}_{n}
and
\check{\textbf{C}}_{n}
are given by

\check{\textbf{B}}(t)=\begin{pmatrix}\textbf{I}_{q\times q}&\bm{0}_{q\times q}\\
\bm{0}_{1\times q}&\textbf{B}(t)\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\check{\textbf{C}}=\begin{pmatrix}\textbf{C}&\bm{0}_{q\times q}\\
\bm{0}_{q\times q}&\textbf{C}\end{pmatrix},

t\in\mathcal{T}
, because from
\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lVert\textbf{B}_{n}(t)-\textbf{B}(t)\rVert=o_{p}(1)
and
\lVert\textbf{C}_{n}-\textbf{C}\rVert=o_{p}(1)
, it follows that
\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lVert\check{\textbf{B}}_{n}(t)-\check{\textbf{B}}(t)\rVert=o_{p}(1)
and
\lVert\check{\textbf{C}}_{n}-\check{\textbf{C}}\rVert=o_{p}(1)
, respectively. Finally, under
II.2.2
and due to (
II.41
) it follows with
I.2.6
of Part I that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}+\check{\textbf{B}}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}\cdot{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}(\tau),\text{ in }(D(\mathcal{T}))^{(q+1)},

(II.42)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Furthermore, the covariance function of
{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}+\check{\textbf{B}}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}\cdot{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}(\tau)
is given by

t\mapsto\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}(t)+\check{\textbf{B}}(t)\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}\cdot\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}(\tau)\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}^{\top}\cdot\check{\textbf{B}}(t)^{\top},

as
\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{k}},\tilde{\check{g}}}(t)^{\top}=\textbf{V}_{\tilde{\check{g}},\tilde{\check{k}}}(t)=\bm{0}_{2q\times(q+1)}.

For the wild bootstrap counterpart
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})
of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})(\cdot)&=\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{*\top}-\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{\top},\hat{A}^{*}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{*})-\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}))^{\top}\\
&=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}_{q\times 1}&+\textbf{I}_{q\times q}\cdot\textbf{C}^{*}_{n}\cdot\textbf{D}^{*}_{n,g}(\tau)+o_{p}(1)\\
D^{*}_{n,k}(\cdot)&+\textbf{B}^{*}_{n}(\cdot)\cdot\textbf{C}^{*}_{n}\cdot\textbf{D}^{*}_{n,g}(\tau)+o_{p}(1)\end{pmatrix}\\
&={\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n,\check{k}}(\cdot)+\check{\textbf{B}}^{*}_{n}(\cdot)\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}^{*}_{n}\cdot{\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n,\check{g}}(\tau)+o_{p}(1),\end{split}

(II.43)

where
{\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n,\check{k}}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\check{\textbf{k}}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})G_{i}dN_{i}(u)
,
{\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n,\check{g}}(t)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}\check{\textbf{g}}_{n,i}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})G_{i}dN_{i}(u)
,
t\in\mathcal{T}
, with

\check{\textbf{k}}_{n}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})=\begin{pmatrix}\bm{0}_{q\times 1}\\
k_{n}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\check{\textbf{g}}_{n,i}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})=\begin{pmatrix}{\textbf{g}}_{n,i}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})\\
{\textbf{g}}_{n,i}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})\end{pmatrix}.

Additionally,

\check{\textbf{B}}^{*}_{n}(t)=\begin{pmatrix}\textbf{I}_{q\times q}&\bm{0}_{q\times q}\\
\bm{0}_{q\times 1}^{\top}&\textbf{B}^{*}_{n}(t)\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\check{\textbf{C}}^{*}_{n}=\begin{pmatrix}\textbf{C}^{*}_{n}&\bm{0}_{q\times q}\\
\bm{0}_{q\times q}&\textbf{C}^{*}_{n}\end{pmatrix},

t\in\mathcal{T}
, where
{\textbf{B}}^{*}_{n}
and
{\textbf{C}}^{*}_{n}
are defined in (
II.24
) and (
II.38
), respectively.
Note that the structure of the asymptotic representation of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})(\cdot)=\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{*\top}-\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{\top},\hat{A}^{*}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{*})-\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}))^{\top}
resembles the structure of the asymptotic representations of its components
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{*\top}-\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{\top})
and
\sqrt{n}(\hat{A}^{*}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{*})-\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}))
.
Moreover, just like for
{\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n,k}
and
{\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n,g}
, it holds that
{\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n,\check{k}}
and
{\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n,\check{g}}
are square integrable martingales with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{2}
.
Additionally, under
II.2.2

(i)
-
(iv)
and conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)
, it follows with
I.3.6
of Part I
that
({\textbf{D}}^{*\top}_{n,\check{k}},{\textbf{D}}^{*\top}_{n,\check{g}})^{\top}
converge in law to
({\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}^{\top},{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}^{\top})^{\top}
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Furthermore, under
II.2.2
, we have

\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lVert\check{\textbf{B}}^{*}_{n}(t)-\check{\textbf{B}}(t)\rVert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0\quad\text{ and }\quad\lVert\check{\textbf{C}}^{*}_{n}-\check{\textbf{C}}\rVert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}0,\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,

because
\sup_{t\in\mathcal{T}}\lVert\textbf{B}^{*}_{n}(t)-\textbf{B}(t)\rVert=o_{p}(1)
and
\lVert\textbf{C}^{*}_{n}-\textbf{C}\rVert=o_{p}(1)
. From
II.2.2
and (
II.43
) we conclude by means of
I.3.10
of Part I that, conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}+\check{\textbf{B}}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}\cdot{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}(\tau),\text{ in }(D(\mathcal{T}))^{(q+1)},

(II.44)

in probability as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Comparison of (
II.42
) with (
II.44
) leads to the final conclusion that the (conditional) distributions of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})
and
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}-{\bm{\theta}}_{0})
are asymptotically equivalent, as
n\rightarrow\infty
. This completes the proof of
II.2.8
.

\blacksquare

B.3 Proofs of
Section

II.2.4

Proof of
II.2.9

In order to derive the Hadamard derivative, we consider
\Gamma
as the composition of the following three functionals

\displaystyle\varphi_{Z}:(\bm{x}^{\top},y)^{\top}(t)\mapsto(\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\bm{x}),y(t))^{\top};

\displaystyle\zeta:(x,y)(t)\mapsto x\cdot y(t);

\displaystyle\psi:x(t)\mapsto 1-\exp(-x(t)).

This yields

\Gamma(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)})(t)=1-\exp\big\{-\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j)})\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j)}(t)\big\}=(\psi\circ\zeta\circ\varphi_{Z})(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)})(t),\qquad j=0,1,2,

where

\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}(t)=(\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j)\top},\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j)}(t))^{\top}

with
\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(0)}=\bm{{\beta}}_{0}
,
\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}=\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}
,
\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}=\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{*}_{n}

and
\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(0)}(t)=A_{1;0}(t)
,
\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(1)}(t)=\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})
,
\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(2)}(t)=\hat{A}^{*}_{1;0,n}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{*}_{n})
.
Furthermore, with the chain rule, we obtain for
j=1,2
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{d}\Gamma(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})\cdot\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})(t)\\
&=\text{d}(\psi\circ\zeta\circ\varphi_{Z})({\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}})\cdot\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})(t)\\
&=\text{d}\psi({\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}})))\cdot\text{d}\zeta({\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}}))\cdot\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})\cdot\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})(t).\end{split}

(II.45)

Evaluating the last expression in (
II.45
) step by step, we first get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}})\cdot(\textbf{x}^{\top},y)^{\top}(t)=(\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}{\bm{\theta}}_{1})\textbf{Z}^{\top}\textbf{x},y(t))^{\top}\\
&=(\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)\top})\textbf{Z}^{\top}\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j)}-\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)}),\sqrt{n}(\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j)}(t)-\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j-1)}(t)))^{\top}\end{split}

(II.46)

with
\bm{\theta}=(\bm{\theta}_{1}^{\top},\theta_{2})^{\top}=(\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)\top},\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j-1)}(t))^{\top}
,
\textbf{x}=\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j)}-\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)})
,
y(t)=\sqrt{n}(\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j)}(t)-\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j-1)}(t))
.
Then, with (
II.46
) we find

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{d}\zeta(\bm{\theta})\cdot(x,y)^{\top}(t)=\theta_{2}(t)\cdot x+\theta_{1}\cdot y(t)\\
&=\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j-1)}(t)\cdot\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)})\textbf{Z}^{\top}\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j)}-\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)})\\
&\quad+\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)})\cdot\sqrt{n}(\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j)}(t)-\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j-1)}(t))\end{split}

(II.47)

with
\bm{\theta}=(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})^{\top}={\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}})=(\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)}),\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j-1)}(t))^{\top}
,
(x,y)^{\top}(t)=\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})\cdot\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})(t)
.
Finally, with (
II.47
) we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{d}\psi(\theta)\cdot x(t)=\exp(-\theta(t))\cdot x(t)\\
&=\exp\{-\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)})\cdot\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j-1)}(t)\}\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)})\\
&\quad\cdot\big[\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j-1)}(t)\cdot\textbf{Z}^{\top}\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j)}-\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)})+\sqrt{n}(\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j)}(t)-\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j-1)}(t))\big]\end{split}

(II.48)

with
\theta=\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}))=\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)})\cdot\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j-1)}(t)
,
x(t)=\text{d}\zeta({\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}}))\cdot\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})\cdot\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})(t)
.
Combining (
II.45
) and (
II.48
) yields
II.2.9
.

\blacksquare

For the proof of
II.2.10
we will use, like in Part I, that the probability space can be modelled as a product space
(\Omega,\mathcal{A},\mathbb{P})=(\Omega_{1}\times\Omega_{2},\mathcal{A}_{1}\otimes\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathbb{P}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}_{2})=(\Omega_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{1})\otimes(\Omega_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathbb{P}_{2})
. Where necessary, we will distinguish between the probability space
(\Omega_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathbb{P}_{1})
underlying the data sets
\{\mathbbm{1}\{C_{i}\geq t\},N_{i}(t),Y_{i}(t),\textbf{Z}_{i},t\in\mathcal{T},i=1,\ldots,n\}
, and the probability space
(\Omega_{2},\mathcal{A}_{2},\mathbb{P}_{2})
underlying the multipliers
G_{1},\dots,G_{n}
. Additionally, we denote by
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{P}_{2}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}
the convergence in law w.r.t. the probability measure
\mathbb{P}_{2}
. Moreover, for some stochastic quantity
\textbf{H}_{n}
, we denote
\textbf{H}_{n}
given the data as
\textbf{H}_{n}|\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)(\omega)
,
\omega\in\Omega_{1}
.

Proof of
II.2.10

We wish to show that the conditional limiting distribution of
\sqrt{n}(\Gamma(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}^{*})-\Gamma(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}))
is asymptotically equivalent to the limiting distribution of
\sqrt{n}(\Gamma(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})-\Gamma({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))
. For this we recall the asymptotic representation (
II.25
) of
\sqrt{n}(\Gamma(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)})-\Gamma(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}))(t)
.
In the proof of
II.2.9
we have introduced the functional
\Gamma
as a composition of the three functionals
\varphi_{Z}
,
\zeta
and
\psi
. For the present proof it is useful to consider the Hadamard derivatives
\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})
,
\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}))
and
\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})))
without directly multiplying them by
\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j)}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})
as we did in (
II.45
) . In particular, we now identify the Hadamard-derivatives with

\displaystyle\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)})\textbf{Z}^{\top}&0\\
\bm{0}_{1\times q}&1\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}))

\displaystyle=\Big(\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})_{2},\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})_{1}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\tilde{A}^{(j-1)}_{1;0}(\cdot),\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)})\Big),

\displaystyle\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})))

\displaystyle=\exp\{-\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})\}

\displaystyle=\exp\{-\exp(\textbf{Z}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)})\cdot\tilde{A}^{(j-1)}_{1;0}(\cdot)\}.

In the above,
\varphi_{Z}(\cdot)_{i}
denotes the i-th component of
\varphi_{Z}
, and
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}=(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{1}^{(j-1)\top},\tilde{\theta}_{2}^{(j-1)})^{\top}=(\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(j-1)\top},\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(j-1)}(\cdot))^{\top}
with
\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(0)}=\bm{{\beta}}_{0}
,
\tilde{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}=\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}
,
\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(0)}(\cdot)=A_{1;0}(\cdot)
and
\tilde{A}_{1;0}^{(1)}(\cdot)=\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(\cdot,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})
.
With the chain rule, we can express the Hadamard derivative
\text{d}\Gamma
of
\Gamma
as follows:

\displaystyle\text{d}\Gamma(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)})=\text{d}\psi({\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}})))\cdot\text{d}\zeta({\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}}))\cdot\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}).

(II.49)

We first consider the case
j=1
. In this case,
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}=\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(0)}=\bm{\theta}_{0}
is a constant point in the space
\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]
, where
\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]^{x}
is the set of all continuous functions mapping from
[0,\tau]
to
\mathbb{R}^{x}
,
x\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus,
\big(\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\big)
is a constant in the space
\mathbb{R}^{2q+2}\times\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]\times\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]\subset\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]^{2q+5}
. We now turn to the second term of the expression on the right-hand side of (
II.25
). For
j=1
we have
\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(1)}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(0)})=\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})
and as formulated in the proof of
II.2.8
it holds that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}-\bm{\theta}_{0})={\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{k}}+\check{\textbf{B}}_{n}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}_{n}\cdot{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{g}}(\tau)+o_{p}(1).

(II.50)

From the proof of
I.2.6
it follows that the convergence in distribution of this term is based on the joint convergence in distribution of
\big({\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{k}}^{\top},{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{g}}^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{B}}_{n})^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{C}}_{n})\big)
to
\big({\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}^{\top},{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{B}})^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{C}})^{\top}\big)
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
, with
\big({\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}^{\top},{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{B}})^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{C}})^{\top}\big)\in\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]^{10q+2}
.
From the continuous mapping theorem and the maps
f_{1},f_{2}
, and
f_{3}
defined in the proof of
I.2.6
it follows that

{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{k}}+\check{\textbf{B}}_{n}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}_{n}\cdot{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{g}}(\tau)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}+\check{\textbf{B}}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}\cdot{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}(\tau),\text{ in }\mathcal{D}[0,\tau]^{(q+1)},\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

In order to derive the convergence in distribution of
\text{d}\Gamma({\bm{\theta}}_{0})\cdot\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}-{\bm{\theta}}_{0})(t)
, we enlarge

\big({\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{k}}^{\top},{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{g}}^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{B}}_{n})^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{C}}_{n})^{\top}\big)

by
\big(\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\big)
. As the first vector converges in distribution to a limit that is continuous and thus separable, and the latter vector is a constant of the space
\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]^{2q+5},

it holds according to Example 1.4.7 of
van der Vaart and Wellner 1996
that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big({\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{k}}^{\top},{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{g}}^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{B}}_{n})^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{C}}_{n})^{\top},\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\big)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\big({\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}^{\top},{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{B}})^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{C}})^{\top},\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\big),\end{split}

(II.51)

in
\mathcal{D}[0,\tau]^{12q+7}
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Next, we make use of the continuous mapping theorem. For this we consider the following map

\displaystyle f_{4}:\;

\displaystyle\big([{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{k}}+\check{\textbf{B}}_{n}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}_{n}\cdot{\textbf{D}}_{n,\check{g}}(\tau)]^{\top},\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big(\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\cdot\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))\cdot\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})\cdot[\textbf{D}_{n,k}+\textbf{B}_{n}\cdot\textbf{C}_{n}\cdot\textbf{D}_{n,g}(\tau)]\big)

Since

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big({\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}^{\top},{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}^{\top},&\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{B}})^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{C}})^{\top},\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\big)\\
&\in\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]^{12q+7},\end{split}

(II.52)

it follows successively with the continuous mapping theorem and the maps
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
,
f_{3}
, and
f_{4}
applied to (
II.51
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\cdot\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))\cdot\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})\cdot[\textbf{D}_{n,k}+\textbf{B}_{n}\cdot\textbf{C}_{n}\cdot\textbf{D}_{n,g}(\tau)]\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\cdot\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))\cdot\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})\cdot[\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}+\check{\textbf{B}}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}\cdot\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}(\tau)],\end{split}

(II.53)

in
D[0,\tau]^{q+1}
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
. In conclusion, (
II.25
), (
II.49
), (
II.50
), and (
II.53
) combined yield

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\Gamma(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})-\Gamma({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{d}\Gamma({\bm{\theta}}_{0})\cdot[\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}+\check{\textbf{B}}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}\cdot\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}(\tau)],\text{ in }\mathcal{D}[0,\tau]^{q+1},\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

(II.54)

This completes the proof for the case
j=1
.

For the case
j=2
, we have
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(j-1)}=\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(1)}=\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}
and

\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\bm{\theta}_{0},\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,

follows from
II.2.8
. Recall that
\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]
holds. Thus,
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}
is asymptotically degenerate. Furthermore,
\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\cdot)
is continuous at every point of the set
\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]
.
Hence, with the continuous mapping theorem as in, e.g., Theorem 1.3.6 of
van der Vaart and Wellner 1996
we get

\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\bm{\theta}_{0}),\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

Moreover,
\varphi_{Z}(\cdot)
is continuous at all points of the space
\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]
mapping the space
\mathbb{R}^{q}\times\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]
to
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]
. Thus, by means of the continuous mapping theorem we have

\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\varphi_{Z}(\bm{\theta}_{0}),\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

Furthermore,
\text{d}\zeta(\cdot)
is a continuous at all points of the space
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]
. Hence, it follows again with the continuous mapping theorem that

\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\bm{\theta}_{0})),\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

Additionally,
\zeta(\cdot)
is continuous at all points of the set
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]
and maps the space
\mathbb{R}\times\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]
to
\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]
. This yields

\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\bm{\theta}_{0})),\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,

according to the continuous mapping theorem. Finally,
\text{d}\psi(\cdot)
is continuous at all points of the set
\mathcal{C}[0,\tau]
. Hence, with the continuous mapping theorem we get

\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\bm{\theta}_{0}))),\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.

In conclusion,
\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})
,
\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}))
, and
\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})))
are asymptotically degenerate. It immediately follows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big(\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})))\big)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{P}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\big(\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\big),\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty.\end{split}

(II.55)

By means of the notation introduced just outside the proof of
II.2.10
, by Fact 1 of the supplement of
Dobler et al. 2019
, which states that convergence in probability is equivalent to convergence in conditional probability, and by the subsequence principle, we can infer from (
II.55
) that for every subsequence
n_{1}
of
n
there exists a further subsequence
n_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big(\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{2}}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{2}})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{2}})))\big)|\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)(\omega)\\
&{\longrightarrow}\big(\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\big),\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,\end{split}

(II.56)

for
\mathbb{P}_{1}
-almost all
\omega\in\Omega_{1}
. Moreover, for
j=2
, we have
\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(2)}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{(1)})=\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})
for which it follows according to the proof of
II.2.8
that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}^{*}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})={\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n,\check{k}}+\check{\textbf{B}}^{*}_{n}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}^{*}_{n}\cdot{\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n,\check{g}}(\tau)+o_{p}(1).

(II.57)

According to the proof of
I.3.10
, we know that
({\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n_{6},\check{k}},{\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n_{6},\check{g}},\check{\textbf{B}}^{*}_{n_{6}},\check{\textbf{C}}^{*}_{n_{6}})|\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)(\omega)
converges in
\mathbb{P}_{2}
-law to
({\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}},{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}},\check{\textbf{B}},\check{\textbf{C}})
for
\mathbb{P}_{1}
-almost all
\omega\in\Omega_{1}
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Additionally, by means of the continuous mapping theorem and the maps
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
, and
f_{3}
, which are defined in that proof, it follows that

\displaystyle{\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n_{6},\check{k}}+\check{\textbf{B}}^{*}_{n_{6}}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}^{*}_{n_{6}}\cdot{\textbf{D}}^{*}_{n_{6},\check{g}}(\tau)|\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)(\omega)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{P}_{2}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}+\check{\textbf{B}}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}\cdot{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}(\tau),\text{ in }\mathcal{D}[0,\tau]^{(q+1)},

(II.58)

as
n\rightarrow\infty
and for
\mathbb{P}_{1}
-almost all
\omega\in\Omega_{1}
. Clearly, the convergence in (
II.56
) and (
II.58
) holds along a joint subsequence
n_{8}
as well. We also have that the limit in law with respect to
\mathbb{P}_{2}
of
({\textbf{D}}^{*\top}_{n_{8},\check{k}},{\textbf{D}}^{*\top}_{n_{8},\check{g}},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{B}}^{*}_{n_{8}})^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{C}}^{*}_{n_{8}})^{\top})|\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)(\omega)
is separable for
\mathbb{P}_{1}
-almost all
\omega\in\Omega
and
\big(\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{8}}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{8}})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{8}})))\big)|\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)(\omega)
is asymptotically degenerate. Therefore, we can conclude based on Example 1.4.7 of
van der Vaart and Wellner 1996
that, conditionally on

\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)(\omega)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big({\textbf{D}}^{*\top}_{n_{8},\check{k}},{\textbf{D}}^{*\top}_{n_{8},\check{g}},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{B}}^{*}_{n_{8}})^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{C}}^{*}_{n_{8}})^{\top},\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{8}}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{8}})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{8}})))\big)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{P}_{2}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\big({\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}^{\top},{\textbf{D}}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{B}})^{\top},\text{vec}(\check{\textbf{C}})^{\top},\text{vec}(\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))^{\top},\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})),\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\big),\end{split}

(II.59)

in
\mathcal{D}[0,\tau]^{12q+7}
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
and for
\mathbb{P}_{1}
-almost all
\omega\in\Omega_{1}
.
From (
II.52
), the continuous mapping theorem, and application of the maps
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
,
f_{3}
, and
f_{4}
to (
II.59
) it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{8}})))\cdot\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{8}}))\cdot\text{d}\varphi_{Z}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n_{8}})\cdot[\textbf{D}^{*}_{n_{8},k}+\textbf{B}^{*}_{n_{8}}\cdot\textbf{C}^{*}_{n_{8}}\cdot\textbf{D}^{*}_{n_{8},g}(\tau)]|\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)(\omega)\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{P}_{2}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{d}\psi(\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})))\cdot\text{d}\zeta(\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))\cdot\text{d}\varphi_{Z}({\bm{\theta}}_{0})\cdot[\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}+\check{\textbf{B}}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}\cdot\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}(\tau)],\end{split}

(II.60)

in
\mathcal{D}[0,\tau]^{(q+1)}
, as
n\rightarrow\infty
and for
\mathbb{P}_{1}
-almost all
\omega\in\Omega_{1}
. Eventually, by invoking the subsequence principle again and combining (
II.25
), (
II.49
), (
II.57
), and (
II.60
), we find that, conditionally on
\mathcal{F}_{2}(0)
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\Gamma(\hat{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{n})-\Gamma(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathbb{P}_{2}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\text{d}\Gamma({\bm{\theta}}_{0})\cdot[\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{k}}}+\check{\textbf{B}}\cdot\check{\textbf{C}}\cdot\textbf{D}_{\tilde{\check{g}}}(\tau)],\text{ in }\mathcal{D}[0,\tau]^{q+1},\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,

(II.61)

in
\mathbb{P}_{1}
-probability. This completes the proof for the case
j=2
.

As the (conditional) limits in distribution of
\sqrt{n}(\Gamma(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n})-\Gamma({\bm{\theta}}_{0}))
and
\sqrt{n}(\Gamma(\hat{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{n})-\Gamma(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}))
in (
II.54
) and (
II.61
), respectively, are the same, we have proved

II.2.10
.

\blacksquare

B.4 Proofs of
Section

II.3

Proof of
II.3.1

We first show that under
II.2.2
,
\hat{\sigma}^{2}_{n}(t)
defined in (
II.27
) is a consistent estimator of the variance of
W_{n,\phi,1}(t)
for
t\in\mathcal{T}
. For this, we point out that

\displaystyle\begin{split}W_{n,\phi,1}(t)&=\sqrt{n}(\textbf{Z}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}-\bm{{\beta}}_{0})+\log(\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}))-\log(A_{1;0}(t)))\\
&=\sqrt{n}(\textbf{Z}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}-\bm{{\beta}}_{0})+\log^{\prime}(A_{1;0}(t))(\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-A_{1;0}(t)))+o_{p}(1).\end{split}

(II.62)

From
II.2.4
and (
II.37
) we see that under
II.2.2
, the asymptotic covariance matrix of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}-\bm{{\beta}}_{0})
equals
C
.
Moreover, in view of (
II.37
) we have that
\lVert\textbf{I}_{n}(\tau,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-\textbf{I}_{n}(\tau,\bm{\beta}_{0})\rVert=o_{p}(1)
, since
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}
is a consistent estimator of
\bm{\beta}_{0}
. Hence,
\big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{I}_{n}(\tau,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})\big)^{-1}
is a consistent estimator of the covariance of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}-\bm{{\beta}}_{0})
, cf. Corollary VII.2.4 of
Andersen et al. 1993
.
Next,
from
II.2.5

it is easy to see that under
II.2.2
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}S^{(0)}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})^{-1}d\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\textbf{E}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})^{\top}d\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})\big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{I}_{n}(\tau,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})\big)^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}\textbf{E}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})d\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}),

is a uniformly consistent estimator of the variance function of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-A_{1;0}(t))
,
t\in\mathcal{T}
.
As according to
II.2.4
and
II.2.5
, and due to the asymptotic orthogonality of
D_{\tilde{k}}
and
\textbf{D}_{\tilde{g}}
, it holds that under
II.2.2
, the covariance function of
\textbf{C}\cdot\textbf{D}_{\tilde{g}}(\tau)
and
D_{\tilde{k}}+\textbf{B}\cdot\textbf{C}\cdot\textbf{D}_{\tilde{g}}(\tau)
equals
\textbf{C}\cdot\textbf{B}^{\top}
. Hence, we find that

-\Big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{I}_{n}(\tau,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})\Big)^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}\textbf{E}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})d\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}),

t\in\mathcal{T},

is a uniformly consistent estimator of the covariance of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}-\bm{{\beta}}_{0})
and
\sqrt{n}(\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})-A_{1;0}(t))
.
Combining this with (
II.62
), we see that under
II.2.2
,
\hat{\sigma}^{2}_{n}(t)
defined in (
II.27
) is a consistent estimator of the variance of
W_{n,\phi,1}(t)
,
t\in\mathcal{T}
.

We now consider the wild bootstrapped variance estimator
\hat{\sigma}^{*2}_{n}(t)
,
t\in\mathcal{T}
, from (
II.28
). According to
II.2.8
, under
II.2.2
the (conditional) covariance functions of
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}^{*}_{n}(t)-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}(t))
and
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{n}(t)-\bm{\theta}_{0}(t))
coincide asymptotically.
Thus, we use the same general structure for
\hat{\sigma}^{*2}_{n}(t)
as given in (
II.27
) for
\hat{\sigma}^{2}_{n}(t)
.
Yet, we replace the basic estimator
\big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{I}_{n}(\tau,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})\big)^{-1}
by the wild bootstrap counterpart
\big(\frac{1}{n}\bm{I}^{*}_{n}(\tau,\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{*}_{n})\big)^{-1}
with

\bm{I}_{n}^{*}(\tau,\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{*}_{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{\tau}(\bm{Z}_{i}-\bm{E}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{*}_{n}))^{\otimes 2}G_{i}^{2}dN_{i}(u),

which is the optional covariation process

[\textbf{D}^{*}_{n,g}](\tau)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{\tau}(\bm{Z}_{i}-\bm{E}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}))^{\otimes 2}G_{i}^{2}dN_{i}(u)

of
\textbf{D}^{*}_{n,g}(t)
at
t=\tau
with
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}
replaced by
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{*}
.
We also replace the basic estimator

\int_{0}^{t}S^{(0)}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})^{-1}d\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})

by the wild bootstrap counterpart

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}S^{(0)}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{*}_{n})^{-2}G_{i}^{2}dN_{i}(u),\quad t\in\mathcal{T},

which originates from the optional covariation process

[D_{n,k}^{*}](t)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t}S^{(0)}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})^{-2}G_{i}^{2}dN_{i}(u)

of
D_{n,k}^{*}(t)
,
t\in\mathcal{T}
, with again
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}
replaced by
\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n}^{*}
.
Note that according to
II.2.3

(i)
in combination with
I.3.7
of Part I, under
II.2.2
the optional covariation processes of
\textbf{D}^{*}_{n,g}
and
D^{*}_{n,k}
converge in probability to
\textbf{V}_{\tilde{g}}
and
V_{\tilde{k}}
, respectively. Therefore, the corresponding wild bootstrap estimators are consistent estimators. For the particular form of the respective optional covariation process we refer to
I.3.2
of Part I. Additionally, we substitute
\hat{A}_{1;0,n}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})
and
\textbf{E}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}_{n})
in (
II.27
) by
\hat{A}^{*}_{1;0,n}(t,\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{*}_{n})
and
\textbf{E}_{n}(u,\hat{\bm{\beta}}^{*}_{n})
, respectively.
All in all, we have that under
II.2.2
,
\hat{\sigma}^{*2}_{n}(t)
defined in (
II.28
)
is a consistent wild bootstrap estimator for the variance of
W_{n,\phi,1}(t)
,
t\in\mathcal{T}
.
This completes the proof of
II.3.1
.

\blacksquare
